# Volume IV — anchors

Every number the Volume IV briefs (`plans/vol-4.md`) quote, with the definition that reproduces it. Each module's
`data/vol4_anchors_lmNN.py` prints its rows (`PYTHONUTF8=1 uv run python data/vol4_anchors_lm19.py`), after
`data/vol3_series.py` (TR23), `data/vol2_series.py` (U28, through `data/lm10_series.py`), `data/build_series.py` (the
0050 and 00631L samples) and `data/vol4_series.py` (TX23) have run; LM26 also reads `data/derived/vol4_rv.csv` and,
for intraday detail and the 2025 TX minute bars, the raw files under `data/raw/intraday/`. IDs are global (A19-01 …
A26-nn) and are cited the same way in the briefs, the reviews and the numbers scripts; each module's
`data/lmNN_numbers.py` compares its values with these rows and reports a difference rather than forcing it. A withdrawn
anchor keeps its row. From Stage 3 the module sources are authoritative (preface of `plans/vol-4.md`).

## LM19

**Status.** Computed by the drafter on 2026-10-19 (`data/vol4_anchors_lm19.py`, run with the locked environment:
NumPy 2.5.3, SciPy 1.18.1, statsmodels 0.15.0); recomputed independently on 2026-10-19 from these definitions (all 68
reproduced except A19-56's median, which broke the quantile rule); revised on 2026-10-19 after the cross-check and the
editor's decisions (`plans/vol-4.md`): A19-56 corrected, definitions tightened, every printed form checked at its
printed precision, A19-69 added. The Value column is the revised script's output.

**Common definitions.** Sample TR23: `data/processed/vol3_taiex.parquet` (built and checked by `data/vol3_series.py`;
FinMind `TaiwanStockTotalReturnIndex`, data_id TAIEX, retrieved 2026-10-18), 5,850 closes 2003-01-02 → 2026-10-08,
$T=5{,}849$ simple returns $R_t$ (the parquet's `R`) from 2003-01-03; log returns $r_t=\ln(1+R_t)$. "2003–2025" is the
5,664 log returns dated 2003-01-03 → 2025-12-31 (calendar years 2003–2025, 2003 from its first close); the limit regimes
are the returns dated before 2015-06-01 (3,077) and from it (2,772). TR23's 23 Saturday sessions stay in every sample
and group. One-day statistics use $R$; sums, autocorrelations and variance ratios use $r$ (and $\lvert r\rvert$, $r^2$).
Divisors: means, $s$, central moments, autocovariances and the standard deviation of period sums divide by the number
of terms; $s_{\mathrm u}$ by $T-1$; the adjusted skewness and kurtosis are SciPy's `bias=False` values. Quantiles:
every quantile and every median, of returns or of computed values, is $\hat F^{-1}(u)=x_{(\lceil un\rceil)}$ of the $n$
values (`method="inverted_cdf"`): the median $\operatorname{med}$, MAD (the median of $\lvert R_t-\operatorname{med}\rvert$),
a 246-day window's median and quartiles, the median of X7's 246 grids. "1.4826 MAD" and "IQR/1.349" are computed
with the exact constants, $\mathrm{MAD}/\Phi^{-1}(0.75)$ and $\mathrm{IQR}/(2\Phi^{-1}(0.75))$; the labels show them
rounded. $\hat\rho_k$ uses the full-sample mean and divisor $T$ in numerator and denominator (statsmodels' `acf`).
$\operatorname{VR}(m)$ is the overlapping estimator (19.8), the book's one variance ratio (AUTHORING §6): every window
sum centred at $m\bar r$, divided by the $T-m+1$ windows, over $m s_r^2$. The non-overlapping $\operatorname{VR}_{\mathrm n}(m)$
skips the first $o$ returns ($0\le o<m$; "from the first return" is $o=0$) and uses the $\lfloor(T-o)/m\rfloor$
consecutive blocks that follow, centred at $m\bar r$ of the whole sample, divisor the number of blocks. The "identity
column" is (19.7) evaluated with the sample $\hat\rho_k$, $1+2\sum_{k<m}(1-k/m)\hat\rho_k$. A "standard deviation of
block sums" is about the blocks' own mean (LM15's convention for the calendar years). $A=T/\text{years}=246.12$ for TR23
(years = calendar days from the first to the last close ÷ 365.25); on 2003–2025 a year is 246 sessions (LM17's), 51.52
ISO weeks (1,185 ÷ 23) or 12 months. Sharpe ratios use $r_f=0$. Counts on returns use `Fraction` on the closes; counts
on computed statistics use the guard (`>= x - 1e-12`, `<= x + 1e-12`) and report their smallest margin. A relative
change is (new − old)/|old|. Seeds: one experiment, $e=1$, `default_rng([1, 20261011])` created once, 10,000 iid
standard normal samples of 5,849 days, one `standard_normal(T)` call per sample; the standard error of a mean of $n$
draws is their standard deviation (divisor $n$) over $\sqrt n$ (LM18's rule). Rounding: the script passes every value
the brief prints from a line, at its printed precision, and ends the line with "[near boundary …]" for each one within
a tenth of a unit of the last printed digit from a rounding boundary (distance in units, truncated); the Printed-as
column repeats each flag. Every printed form is rounded from the unrounded value, never from a figure in this table.
Numbers printed by earlier modules are echoed with their file:line and recomputed here where the anchor says so. The
Value column is the script's line after its ID, verbatim, except that a `|` is written `\|` for the table.

## LM20

**Status.** Revised 2026-10-19 after the cross-check (`check2/cross.md`) and the independent recomputation
(`check2/recompute_lm20.md`, which reproduced all 50 drafted anchors). Computed by `data/vol4_anchors_lm20.py` under the
locked NumPy 2.5.3 and SciPy 1.18.1; output kept beside this file as `anchors_output_rev.txt`; two runs give identical
output. Changes: A20-17's and A20-35's draws are defined by array shape and axis; A20-35's quantiles use
$\hat F^{-1}$ (`inverted_cdf`) with batch standard errors, and its $t$-block standard deviations lose their invalid
Monte Carlo errors to A20-51 (new, experiment 4); A20-32 is withdrawn (X32); A20-28 and A20-29 carry the delta table's
three-decimal standard errors (X2); A20-46 writes $\hat\theta$ (X5); every printed form is now checked at its printed
precision. IDs are stable: a withdrawn anchor keeps its row and its script line.

**Rounding flag.** A value is marked (!) when one of its printed forms lies within a tenth of a unit of the last
printed digit of a rounding boundary ($\lvert x10^d-\lfloor x10^d\rfloor-0.5\rvert<0.1$ at its $d$ printed decimals).
The script's helper checks every printed form at its own precision; the "Printed as" column marks the flagged form.
Each printed form is rounded from the unrounded value, and a separate check (`check_printed.py`, 236 printed forms)
found every one correct.

**Common definitions.** Sample TR23: `data/processed/vol3_taiex.parquet` (built and checked by `data/vol3_series.py`;
FinMind `TaiwanStockTotalReturnIndex`, data_id TAIEX, retrieved 2026-10-18), 5,850 closes 2003-01-02 → 2026-10-08,
$T=5{,}849$ simple returns $R_t$ (the parquet's `R`) from 2003-01-03; log returns $r_t=\ln(1+R_t)$. Years
$\tau=8{,}680/365.25=23.7645$; $A=T/\tau=246.12$; a year on 2003–2025 is LM17's 246 sessions. Moments, skewness
($m_3/s^3$) and kurtosis ($m_4/s^4$) divide by $T$ (or by a part's count); $s_{\mathrm u}$ divides by $T-1$; `ddof` is
passed explicitly; a standard deviation across simulated samples or runs divides by their number. One-day statistics
on $R$, sums (Merton's $m$-day sums, calendar years 2003–2025 with 2003 from its first close) on $r$. Annualized:
$A\bar R$, $\sqrt A\,s$, $\sqrt A\,\bar R/s$ ($r_f=0$; the last two are LM19's estimates, recomputed); the plug-in
Kelly fraction $\hat f=\bar R/s^2$ is daily. Intervals: estimate ± 1.96 SE. Standard errors assume iid days; the delta
method uses $\Var(s^2)\approx(m_4-s^4)/T$, $\Cov(\bar R,s^2)\approx m_3/T$ (sample moments, "plug-in") or normal
moments ($m_3=0$, $m_4=3s^4$). Student-t: `stats.t.fit(R)` refined by Nelder–Mead in percent; SEs from the inverse
central-difference Hessian, checked at a second step size. Counts use the guard (`R <= -x + 1e-12`,
`R >= x - 1e-12`, `R > 1e-12`) and are asserted equal to `Fraction` counts on the two-decimal closes. Seeds:
experiment $e$ creates `rng = default_rng([e, 20261011])` once and never reseeds: $e=1$ the sampling distributions,
$e=2$ the coverage table, $e=3$ Problem 11, $e=4$ the spread of experiment 1's $t$ block; the draws' shapes are in
the rows. Quantiles of simulated values are $\hat F^{-1}(u)$ (`np.quantile(..., method="inverted_cdf")`) with LM18's
batch standard errors. U28: `data/lm10_series.py` ($T=4{,}382$, price indices). LM4's 0050 sample:
`etf_daily_returns.parquet`, `R_0050` ($T=2{,}902$). Earlier modules' values (LM4, LM13–LM19) are recomputed and cited
to their owners. The Value column is the script's line after its ID, verbatim.

## LM21

**Status.** Computed by the Stage 1 drafter on 2026-10-19 with `data/vol4_anchors_lm21.py`; recomputed by a fresh agent from the definitions and the data (all 60 reproduced); revised 2026-10-19 after the cross-check and that recomputation: definitions tightened (A21-31, A21-37, A21-53, A21-20's label), A21-17 and A21-37 changed, A21-61 added (61 rows, none withdrawn). ruff and ty pass; two runs gave identical output. Every printed form below goes through the script's `chk`, which asserts that the unrounded value rounds to it at its own precision and reports on stderr each one within a tenth of a last-digit unit of a rounding boundary. The run reports these, each flagged in its row: A21-03 (23.7645 and 23.76, exact), A21-12 (3.96, from data), A21-13 (3.68, from data), A21-15 and A21-28 (December's 1.53; July's 1.00 and August's −0.50 in X5, from data), A21-23 (the four expected counts at two decimals and LM13's 1,195.0, exact; E6's 5,848 × 0.02143² = 2.69 from printed inputs), A21-31 (the standard error 0.004, Monte Carlo), A21-35 (3.88, exact constant), A21-47 (0.00556 = 0.05/9, exact) and A21-53 (LM15's printed 4.95 and 5.16). Each is printed from its unrounded value or is exact; no printed form is an exact tie.

**Common definitions.** Sample TR23: `data/processed/vol3_taiex.parquet` (built and checked by `data/vol3_series.py`; FinMind `TaiwanStockTotalReturnIndex`, data_id TAIEX, retrieved 2026-10-18), 5,850 closes 2003-01-02 → 2026-10-08, $T=5{,}849$ simple returns $R_t=P_t/P_{t-1}-1$ (the parquet's `R`) from 2003-01-03; no log returns. Owners: $\bar R$, $s$ and the Sharpe ratio are [[LM19]]'s statistics, $\operatorname{SE}(\bar R)$ and the interval for the mean [[LM20]]'s; they are anchored here because LM21 quotes them, and the brief cites their owners. Divisors: $s$ with $T$ (the Sharpe ratio, $r_f=0$; every printed "standard deviation"), $s_{\mathrm u}$ with $T-1$ (every t-stat), `ddof` explicit; a block's variance with 292, skewness and kurtosis with $T$. Years = calendar days ÷ 365.25 = 23.7645 (exactly $4\times8{,}680/1{,}461$); $A=T/\text{years}=246.12$, used only to annualize a Sharpe ratio. The t-stat of a mean is (21.1), $\bar R/(s_{\mathrm u}/\sqrt T)$; the two-sample statistic (21.3) uses unpooled divisor-$(n-1)$ variances (SciPy's `ttest_ind(..., equal_var=False)` is printed beside it as a check). p-values are two-sided against the standard normal unless the row says otherwise. Regimes by return date: before 2015-06-01 and from it. The pair space is LM13's 5,848 pairs; down means $R<0$; a fall is $R\le-3\%$; LM15's blocks are the first 5,840 returns in 292 blocks of 20, an up day $R>0$. The calendar family: the 12 months and the 5 weekdays (Monday–Friday) of the return's date, each against all other TR23 returns; the 23 Saturday sessions belong to every "rest". Guard: `R > 1e-12`, `R <= -0.03 + 1e-12`, statistics against a threshold `>= x - 1e-12`; every return count is asserted equal to a `Fraction` count from the two-decimal closes; constructed p-values are compared as `Fraction`s. Seeds: `np.random.default_rng([e, 20261011])`, created once per experiment: $e=8$ (E8: 10,000 permutations `perm = permutation(T)`; the shuffled series is `R[perm]` placed on TR23's dates in their order, so every month and weekday keeps its dates and size), $e=10$ (E10 and X7: 10,000 rules drawn in one call `integers(0, 2, size=(10000, T), dtype=np.int8)`, rule $i$ row $i$ with position $2x-1$, processed in chunks of 500), $e=11$ (Problem 11, `standard_normal((10000, 17))`). Monte Carlo errors: a simulated share $\hat p$ of $B$ draws has the standard error $\sqrt{\hat p(1-\hat p)/B}$ ((18.2) on indicators); a mean of $B$ draws $s/\sqrt B$; E10's standard deviation has [[LM18]]'s batch standard error (the 20 chunks of 500, $s$ of the 20 values with divisor 20, over $\sqrt{20}$). LM15's and LM16's printed numbers are echoed with file:line; statistics computed from LM16's printed U28 counts are marked so, and A21-61 recounts those counts on U28 (`data/lm10_series.py`). The Value column is the script's line after its ID, verbatim.

**Facts checked on official sources.** Each item names its entry in `project/SOURCES.md` or the drafter's own check.

- Benjamini and Hochberg (1995), abstract: the FDR equals the FWER when every null is true, and the procedure is proved for independent test statistics (SOURCES, Papers and books (Volume IV), LM21).
- Holm (1979), first page read in a JSTOR copy: a prescribed level of protection against errors of the first kind for any combination of true hypotheses (SOURCES, LM21).
- Harvey, Liu and Zhu (2016), abstract: "a t-statistic greater than 3.0" (SOURCES, LM21); the arithmetic of A21-34 is the drafter's own.
- `statsmodels.stats.multitest.multipletests` defaults to Holm–Šidák; `scipy.stats.false_discovery_control` gives BH and BY (SOURCES, Python (Volume IV)); A21-32 and A21-56 run both.
- SciPy's `ttest_ind` (default `equal_var=True`), `chi2_contingency` (default `correction=True`), `binomtest`, `ttest_1samp` and `jarque_bera`: signatures read with `inspect.signature` in the locked SciPy 1.18.1 by the drafter, and `ttest_ind(..., equal_var=False)` and `chi2_contingency` with and without `correction` reproduce A21-14, A21-18 and A21-23. To be recorded in SOURCES "Python (Volume IV)" before Stage 2 (cross-check X34).
- Not yet checked, so the brief writes them as the editor's decisions say: the $t_{T-1}$ law of (21.1) for normal returns (Blitzstein and Hwang §10.4, to be read: *stated without proof* until then, X32); a Jarque–Bera paper (Problem 9 is dropped if none is verified, X34); an official calendar page for TR23's 23 Saturday sessions (A21-07; the text gives no reason for them until one is in SOURCES).

## LM22

**Status.** Computed with `data/vol4_anchors_lm22.py` (about three minutes; NumPy 2.5.3, SciPy 1.18.1, arch 8.0.0);
recomputed independently by a fresh agent on 2026-10-19 (all 50 rows reproduced to every digit); revised on
2026-10-19 to the editor's decisions (`plans/vol-4.md`, "Editor's decisions on the LM19–LM25 drafts") and the
recomputation's findings. Every line now ends with "-> printed" and the forms the brief prints, each rounded from the
unrounded value by the script's `pr()`, which flags a form within a tenth of a unit of its last digit from a rounding
boundary. "(flagged)" in the Printed-as column marks those forms; each is correctly rounded. The draws, and so every
unrounded value, are those of the first run. No anchor is withdrawn or added; IDs are stable.

**Common definitions.**

- **TR23:** `data/processed/vol3_taiex.parquet` (built by `data/vol3_series.py`; FinMind
  `TaiwanStockTotalReturnIndex`, data_id TAIEX, retrieved 2026-10-18), $T=5{,}849$ simple returns
  $R_t=\mathrm{tr}_t/\mathrm{tr}_{t-1}-1$ from 2003-01-03, log returns $r_t=\ln(1+R_t)$, years = calendar days ÷
  365.25, $A=T/\text{years}=246.12$; the 7% regime is the returns dated before 2015-06-01, the 10% regime the rest.
- **U28:** `u28()` of `data/lm10_series.py` (closes repaired by `data/vol2_series.py`; FinMind `TaiwanStockPrice`,
  retrieved 2026-10-11), 28 sector price indices, $T=4{,}382$ returns from 2008-12-02, $A=245.48$; no dividends; a
  calendar year's returns are those dated in it.
- **Divisors:** $s$ and every moment by $T$ (or by the count used); a correlation by the same count in numerator and
  denominator; $s_{\mathrm u}$ by $T-1$, only in the t-stat. $\hat\rho_k$ is LM19's (19.5) (full-sample mean, divisor
  $T$); $\operatorname{VR}(m)$ is LM19's (19.8) (overlapping $m$-day log-return sums centred at $m\bar r$, over
  $m s_r^2$), recomputed in each resample from the resample's own $\bar r$ and $s_r$.
- **Estimates:** Sharpe ratio $\bar R/s$ per day with $r_f=0$, annualized by $\sqrt A$ ($A$ fixed at the sample's
  value); $\hat f=\bar R/s^2$; $\hat f^*$ = the root of (4.5), the mean of $R/(1+fR)$ = 0, by Newton's method from
  $\hat f$ inside its bracket ($1+fR>0$ on every day); volatility $s\sqrt A$.
- **Drawing resamples:** $B=10{,}000$ unless stated, drawn in chunks of 500 rows; a chunk is only how the draws are
  made. iid: `integers(0, n, size=(500, n))` per chunk (SciPy's own draws with `batch=500`); experiments 8–15 draw one
  resample at a time, `integers(0, n, size=n)`, which is the same stream. Stationary, mean length $\ell$: per
  resample `integers(n, size=n)` then `random(n)`; a block starts at position 1 and wherever the uniform is at most
  $1/\ell$; inside a block the index steps by one with wrap-around (arch's draws exactly, A22-42). Moving and circular
  blocks as A22-25.
- **Summaries of $B$ values:** SE = their standard deviation, divisor $B$. Every quantile is $\hat F^{-1}(u)$, the
  order statistic $\lceil uB\rceil$ (`method="inverted_cdf"`): the 95% percentile ends are the 250th and 9,750th of
  10,000; basic = $2\hat\theta$ minus the ends, reversed. "sd of t" divides by $B$; the "95% point of $\lvert t\rvert$"
  is the 9,500th order statistic. Bias = mean of the $B$ values minus the estimate.
- **Monte Carlo errors (LM18's batch rule, lm18.md:153):** the $B$ values in resample order are split into 20 batches
  of $B/20$ consecutive resamples (500 for $B=10{,}000$, 100 for 2,000, 50 for 1,000); the statistic is computed on
  each batch (a standard deviation with divisor $B/20$; a percentile end as the batch's order statistic
  $\lceil0.025B/20\rceil$ or $\lceil0.975B/20\rceil$, the 13th and 488th of 500); the batch SE is the standard deviation
  of the 20 values, divisor 20, over $\sqrt{20}$. A bootstrap p-value's Monte Carlo SE is $\sqrt{p(1-p)/B}$. A ratio of
  two bootstrap SEs from independent experiments has relative Monte Carlo SE equal to the root sum of squares of their
  relative batch SEs.
- **Block lengths (the book's rule):** the largest Politis–White stationary estimate (arch `optimal_block_length`,
  column `stationary`) among the squared demeaned series the statistic uses, rounded: 145 (TR23), 130 (U28 pair), 7
  and 18 (2017, 2020), 108 (7% regime), 36 (10% regime).
- **Counts:** the U28 event uses the guard `>= 2 - 1e-12` (strict count equal, asserted); the windows under water are
  recounted with `Fraction` on the two-decimal closes; bootstrap counts compare continuous values (no ties, asserted).
- **Seeds:** `np.random.default_rng([e, 20261011])` once per experiment: 1 E1; 2 iid TR23; 3 stationary TR23; 4 X6's
  grid; 5, 6 moving and circular; 7 X5; 8, 9 crash; 10–13 PC1; 14, 15 windows; 16–18 tests; 19 KC §8; 20 arch check;
  21 Problem 6; 22, 23 Problem 11; 24 the widget's histograms (`data/lm22_charts.py`, no anchor).
- **Value column:** the script's line after its ID, verbatim.

## LM23

**Status.** Revised 2026-10-19 after the cross-check (`check2/cross.md`) and the independent recomputation
(`check2/recompute_lm23.md`, which reproduced all 58 rows of the draft). `data/vol4_anchors_lm23.py`, 58 rows, run with
the locked NumPy 2.5.3, SciPy 1.18.1 and statsmodels 0.15.0; its output is kept beside the brief and pasted verbatim
below. No row is withdrawn or added. Changed values: A23-25 (residual root mean square by tercile, cut rule stated:
0.260, 0.179, 0.378, was an SD about each tercile's mean, 0.233, 0.024, 0.373), A23-29 (LM19's $\hat\rho_k$: 0.538 and
0.093 at lags 10 and 19, were 0.541 and 0.094), A23-36 (contracts on TX23's $F_t=49{,}357$: 10.13, 8.34, 3.12, were
10.14, 8.35, 3.13 on the TAIEX close), A23-43 (0.696, was 0.6965: 69,645/100,000 is an exact tie at four decimals),
A23-04 (2026's 185 returns, was a misprinted 181; the unused $n=10{,}000$ dropped). Values added to rows: the $t_{183}$
excess 0.7% (A23-19), White ÷ classical 3.04 (A23-21), the beta's shift 2.8 classical SEs (A23-23), $F_t$ (A23-36),
the HAC t-stat −0.68 of the weakest loading (A23-47). Implementation-dependent differences (9e-16, 4e-16, −1.1e-15)
became assertions within $10^{-12}$ (A23-45, A23-49).

**Rounding flags.** The script's `f()` checks every printed form at every precision the brief prints it (the Printed as
column) and flags a value within a tenth of a last-digit unit of a rounding boundary ("NEAR … at k dp" at the end of
the Value). Every Printed as entry was checked against the unrounded value at its printed precision; each is rounded
from the unrounded value, never from a rounded one. Flagged printed forms: A23-11 0.0129; A23-12 2.09; A23-13 1.089;
A23-15 2.09; A23-16 1.212; A23-18 1.225 and 1.244; A23-19 1.970 (df 248) and 1.9699;
A23-20 18,824; A23-22 0.187, −0.96, 0.37; A23-24 23.6, 65.3; A23-25 0.0157, 0.378; A23-27 0.0229; A23-28 0.0089,
0.0406; A23-30 0.0106, 0.0610, 0.0509, 0.0159, 0.518; A23-31 0.0129; A23-32 0.99%, −0.18, −0.59, −0.41%; A23-34 1.156,
1.089; A23-35 0.308; A23-36 0.5916, 0.7505, 0.1989, 3.12; A23-38 1.887, 1.8865, 0.0759, 0.0776, 2.47; A23-39 2.09, −1.94, −1.96,
−1.56 in the text and the two-decimal forms that X6 shows (flagged in the row); A23-40 0.00179; A23-41 0.462; A23-43 0.696 (an exact 0.69645) and
0.0015; A23-45 0.9163; A23-46 0.5916, 0.008, 0.7624; A23-47 0.966; A23-48 0.6842, 0.452, 0.6924, 0.4368, −0.033;
A23-49 0.0288; A23-51 0.0264; A23-52 23.6, 65.3; A23-56 0.1709; A23-58 0.43 and 43%.

**Common definitions.** Sample U28 (`data/lm10_series.py`'s `u28()`, reading `data/processed/vol2_closes.parquet` as
built and repaired by `data/vol2_series.py`; FinMind `TaiwanStockPrice`, retrieved 2026-10-11): the 28 leaf TWSE sector
price indices and the TAIEX price index, closes 2008-12-01 → 2026-10-08, simple daily returns between consecutive rows,
$T=4{,}382$ (2008-12-02 → 2026-10-08), years = calendar days ÷ 365.25, $A=T/\text{years}=245.4794$; halves = the first
and last 2,191 returns. Electronic from the same parquet and dates; the factor $R_{\text{el},t}-R_{\text{mkt},t}$ is
*constructed*. LM2's pairs: `data/processed/etf_daily_returns.parquet` (2,902 total-return pairs). TR23:
`data/processed/vol3_taiex.parquet` (its `R` and `Rp` on U28's dates; its log returns for LM18's paths). TX23:
`data/processed/vol4_tx23.parquet` (`data/vol4_series.py`), one value, $F_t$ on 2026-10-08. OLS with an intercept, $d$
coefficients, $s_\varepsilon^2=\text{RSS}/(T-d)$; classical SE $s_\varepsilon^2(X^{\top}X)^{-1}$; White = HC0 (no
degrees-of-freedom factor); HAC = Newey–West, Bartlett weights $1-k/(\ell+1)$, no small-sample correction,
$\ell=\lfloor4(n/100)^{2/9}\rfloor$ (the book's rule) unless stated (9 for $T=4{,}382$, 8 for 2,902, 7 for a half, 4
for a year or 250 returns; 19 and 38 for the overlapping sums). Descriptive means, SDs, variances and correlations
divide by $T$ (`ddof=0`, passed explicitly); slopes of windows are $\Cov/\Var$ with `ddof=0` (equal to OLS).
R-squared is (12.4). Sample autocorrelations are LM19's $\hat\rho_k$; sample quantiles are $\hat F^{-1}(u)$
(`inverted_cdf`). t-stats name their method; t-stats and p-values use the normal reference, two-sided, except A23-16
($t_2$). Intercepts in bp a day; ×$A$ for a year. One-day regressions on simple returns, 20-day sums on log returns.
Counts use the guard (`|t| > 1.96 + 1e-12`, `x <= -0.05 + 1e-12`) and, on the two-decimal TAIEX closes, `Fraction`.
Seeds: experiment 1 of LM23, `default_rng([1, 20261011])`, 100,000 draws (A23-43); LM18's paths keep LM18's
`default_rng([3, 20261011])` (A23-25). Earlier modules' printed numbers are echoed with file:line in the brief; LM2's,
LM16's and LM18's are recomputed here and cited to their modules in the text.

## LM24

**Status.** Computed by the drafter on 2026-10-19 with `data/vol4_anchors_lm24.py`; recomputed by a fresh check the
same day (all 60 reproduced, `check2/recompute_lm24.md`); revised 2026-10-19 after the cross-check and the
recomputation: definitions tightened (recompute items 1–5), every printed form checked for rounding at its printed
precision, owners named (cross-check X1, X3, X19), A24-61 added. The revised script ran twice with identical output;
`ruff check` and `ty check` pass. IDs A24-01 to A24-60 follow the order of first appearance in the Stage 1 brief;
A24-61 was added at the revision and is out of that order. No anchor was withdrawn.

**Common definitions.** Two samples, never mixed in one statistic. TR23: `data/processed/vol3_taiex.parquet` (built
and checked by `data/vol3_series.py`; FinMind `TaiwanStockTotalReturnIndex`, data_id TAIEX, retrieved 2026-10-18),
5,850 closes 2003-01-02 → 2026-10-08, $T=5{,}849$ simple returns $R_t$ (the parquet's `R`); years = calendar days ÷
365.25 = 23.76; $A=T/\text{years}=246.12$. U28: `data/lm10_series.py`'s `u28()` (closes built and repaired by
`data/vol2_series.py`), the 28 leaf sector *price* indices (no dividends), 4,382 simple returns 2008-12-02 →
2026-10-08, $A=245.4794$, used to annualize both of [[LM11]]'s halves (2,191 returns each). Owners: TR23's $A\bar R$
and $\sqrt A\,s$ are [[LM19]]'s; $s/\sqrt T$, the confidence interval, $\hat f$ and its delta-method errors are
[[LM20]]'s; the t-stat, with $s_{\mathrm u}$, and its p-value are [[LM21]]'s; $\hat f^*=4.1381$ is [[LM17]]'s; the
script recomputes them. Divisors: $s$ and every $s_i$ divide by the returns used (`ddof=0`); $s_{\mathrm u}$ (LM21's
t-stat only) by $T-1$; the cross-sectional SD of 28 means divides by 28. Every interval, credible or confidence, uses
$\Phi^{-1}(0.975)=1.959964$, printed 1.96 (lm15.md:194). Normal model: $\sigma=s$, standard error $s/\sqrt T$, prior
$\mathcal N(\mu_0,\sigma_0^2)$ centred at zero unless stated, data's weight $c=\sigma_0^2/(\sigma_0^2+s^2/T)$,
posterior mean $\mu_0+c(\bar R-\mu_0)$, posterior SD $\sqrt c\,s/\sqrt T$; "$A\sigma_0=10$ points" is a prior SD of 10
percentage points on $A\mu$. Kelly: $r_f=0$, $\hat f=\bar R/s^2$, Bayes fraction $f_{\mathrm B}=\E[\mu\mid\text{data}]/s^2$;
"exact" fractions are roots of (4.5) on TR23's returns shifted by one constant to the posterior mean (LM4's band
method); with no shift the root is $\hat f^*$. James–Stein: positive part toward the grand mean,
$1-\hat c=(N-3)\,v/\sum_i(\bar R_i-\bar{\bar R})^2$ with $v$ the average of the 28 $s_i^2/T$; "common removed"
subtracts each day's mean of the 28 returns. Out-of-sample miss: root mean square over the 28 sectors of estimate minus
the other half's mean, annualized. Beta probabilities are exact (`Fraction`, integer parameters) and checked against
SciPy. Counts compare means, never returns, with the guard ($\ge10^{-12}$, $\le-10^{-12}$). Seeds: Experiment 1 (E6)
`default_rng([1, 20261011])`, Experiment 2 (Problem 10) `default_rng([2, 20261011])`, each created once at its start;
the draw order is in A24-42 and A24-57. A Monte Carlo average is printed with its standard error: the SD of the
per-cross-section values with divisor $n$, over $\sqrt n$. "[near a / b]" marks a value within a tenth of a printed
unit of a rounding boundary, checked at every precision in which the brief prints it (the Printed-as column lists
each form). The Value column is the script's line after its ID, verbatim.

## LM25

**Status.** Computed by the Stage 1 drafter on 2026-10-19 with `data/vol4_anchors_lm25.py`; all 69 rows reproduced by
the independent recomputation; revised 2026-10-19 after the cross-check (X9, X13, X16, X24, X37) and the
recomputation: 72 rows, A25-69 withdrawn, A25-70 to A25-72 added. A flag "near" marks a printed value within a tenth
of a unit of a rounding boundary at the precision it is printed (the script's `NEAR(d)`, d the decimals); Printed-as
forms are rounded from the unrounded values.

**Common definitions.** TX23: `data/processed/vol4_tx23.parquet` (built and checked by `data/vol4_series.py`;
FinMind `TaiwanFuturesDaily` TX, regular session, monthly contracts, retrieved 2026-10-19; TAIEX price closes from
`TaiwanStockPrice`, retrieved 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-18), 5,850 dates 2003-01-02 → 2026-10-08: $S_t$ the TAIEX price
close, $F_t$ the settlement of the near-month contract held from that close, rolled at the close of the day before
each contract's last trading day; $b_t=\ln F_t-\ln S_t$, printed in bp ($10^{4}$ log units); rolled changes
$\Delta b_t$ difference the stored series, roll-free changes are `rF − rS`. A window's statistic (a limit regime, a
year) uses only its own dates, and its changes are differences within the window. D: the 5,834 TX23 dates whose
contract's last trading day $L$ is in the sample (to 2026-09-14); realized dividends
$q_{\text{real}}\tau=\ln(\mathrm{TR}_L/\mathrm{TR}_t)-\ln(S_L/S_t)$ from TR23's `tr`; $\tau$ = calendar days to $L$
/ 365; $r_f=2\%$; fair log basis $r_f\tau-q_{\text{real}}\tau$; residual $b_t$ − fair (LM6's (6.25) in log form).
TR23: `data/processed/vol3_taiex.parquet`, log returns $r_t=\ln(1+R_t)$, $T=5{,}849$, 2003-01-03 → 2026-10-08.
Divisors: $s$ by the number of terms; $s_\varepsilon$ by $T-d$; $\hat\rho_k$ with the full-sample mean and divisor
$T$ (LM19's (19.5), statsmodels' `acf`); partial autocorrelations by OLS (`pacf(method="ols")`). Quantiles and
medians, of data and of simulated values, are $\hat F^{-1}(u)$ (`method="inverted_cdf"`). HC0-type
$\operatorname{SE}_{\text{rob}}(\hat\rho_k)=\sqrt{\sum_{t>k}\tilde r_t^2\tilde r_{t-k}^2}/\sum_t\tilde r_t^2$,
$\tilde r_t=r_t-\bar r$. $\operatorname{VR}(m)$ is LM19's (19.8): the mean square of the overlapping $m$-day sums
of $r$ centred at $m\bar r$ (divisor $T-m+1$) over $m$ times the daily variance (divisor $T$); its t-stat (25.6)
divides $\operatorname{VR}(m)-1$ by $\operatorname{SE}_{\text{iid}}=\sqrt{2(2m-1)(m-1)/(3mT)}$ or by
$\operatorname{SE}_{\text{rob}}=\sqrt{\sum_{k<m}[2(1-k/m)]^2\operatorname{SE}_{\text{rob}}(\hat\rho_k)^2}$ (two-sided,
normal reference, large-sample). Tests: two-sided with a normal reference unless named; Dickey–Fuller, ADF and
Engle–Granger one-sided (small values reject), KPSS one-sided (large values reject), Ljung–Box the upper tail of
$\chi^2_K$. Dickey–Fuller: OLS with classical SE (divisor $T-d$); ADF, KPSS, Engle–Granger and Ljung–Box from
statsmodels 0.15.0 (`result_object=True`, `autolag="AIC"`, `nlags="auto"`, `return_results=True`). ARMA by
`ARIMA(order=(p,0,q))` on $10^4b_t$ ("const" = mean) at the likelihood maximum: `method="innovations_mle"`, then a
restart of the state-space optimizer, asserting that the log-likelihood rises by less than $10^{-6}$; standard
errors from $-\ell''$ (`cov_type="approx"`, checked against a central-difference Hessian). Limit regimes: before /
from 2015-06-01. Guard $b_t<-10^{-12}$, checked with `Fraction`. Seeds: `default_rng([e, 20261011])`, $e$ = 1 (DF
null, 10,000 walks of 5,850 levels), 2 (spurious, 1,000 pairs of 5,850), 3 (DF null, 100,000 walks of 246), 4
(spurious, 1,000 pairs of 246). A walk of $n$ levels is $x_t=z_1+\dots+z_t$, $t=1,\dots,n$ (no leading zero): chunks
of 500 rows `standard_normal((500, n))` cumulated along rows; a pair is `standard_normal((2, n))` cumulated along rows,
$x$ then $y$. SE of a share: binomial; of a quantile or median: LM18's batch SE, the standard deviation with divisor
20 of its values on 20 equal consecutive batches, over $\sqrt{20}$.

## LM26

**Status.** Computed by the Stage 1 drafter on 2026-10-19 with `data/vol4_anchors_lm26.py` (66 rows); not yet
recomputed independently. A flag "NEAR(d)" marks a value within a tenth of a unit of a rounding boundary at d decimals,
the precision at which it is printed; Printed-as forms are rounded from the unrounded values.

**Common definitions.** TR23: `data/processed/vol3_taiex.parquet` (`data/vol3_series.py`; FinMind
`TaiwanStockTotalReturnIndex`, data_id TAIEX, retrieved 2026-10-18), $T=5{,}849$ log returns $r_t=\ln(1+R_t)$,
2003-01-03 → 2026-10-08, used in percent ($100r_t$) by every daily model; years = calendar days between the first and
last close ÷ 365.25; $A=T/\text{years}=246.12$. T5S: `data/derived/vol4_rv.csv` (`data/vol4_rv.py`; FinMind
`TaiwanVariousIndicators5Seconds`, retrieved 2026-10-19; TWSE's TAIEX), 5,351 sessions 2005-01-03 → 2026-10-08, the
same dates as TR23 from 2005-01-03; $\mathrm{RV}_t(h)$ the sum of squared log changes on the grid 09:30:00,
09:30:00 + $h$, …, 13:30:00; `o2` $=r_{\mathrm o,t}^2$, previous close → 09:30:00; the target
$V_t=10^4(\texttt{o2}+\texttt{rv\_15m})$ in %². Raw rows ("raw") read `data/raw/intraday/taiex5s/` and, for TX, the
2025 1-minute bars `data/raw/intraday/tx1m/2025/` (FinMind `TaiwanFuturesKBar`) with TX23's held contract of the
previous date; a bar's label is its start, the price at 09:30:00 is the 09:29 bar's close, a minute without a bar keeps
the previous close. Intraday autocorrelations pool within-day pairs, uncentred unless said (centring changes none by
more than 0.0005). GARCH: arch 8.0.0 `arch_model(100r, mean="Constant", vol="GARCH", p=1, q=1, dist=...)`,
`fit(disp="off")`, convergence asserted; arch's backcast (weights $0.94^j$ on the first 75 squared residuals about the
sample mean); the hand log-likelihood equals arch's; classic SE from $-\ell''$, robust from the sandwich. EWMA:
$\lambda=0.94$, zero mean, started at the mean square of the first 246 returns. Out of sample: $W=1{,}231$, refits at
each month's first session on the previous 1,231 returns, the recursion restarted at the window's start; forecasts
2008-01-02 → 2026-10-08 (4,609 days); MSE $(V-f)^2$ in %⁴, QLIKE $V/f-\ln(V/f)-1$; Newey–West with Bartlett weights,
$\lfloor4(n/100)^{2/9}\rfloor$ lags, divisor $n$, no correction; HC0 for Mincer–Zarnowitz. Tests two-sided with a
normal reference, the ARCH test and Ljung–Box upper tails. Quantiles and medians $\hat F^{-1}(u)$
(`method="inverted_cdf"`); the guard $R\le-0.03+10^{-12}$. Seeds `default_rng([e, 20261011])`: $e=1$ normal GARCH,
$e=2$ $t$ GARCH; 1,000 paths of 5,849 days, started at the unconditional variance, one draw per path a day; medians
with LM18's batch SE (20 batches of 50).
